**(E08 plan:)** Wed Sep 30, St. Jerome — Hale opens with Jerome and the widow Paula ("the scandal of Rome; Mr. Costigan's thesis
in a hair shirt"); the week's subject is **lying** (Augustine, *De mendacio*; Kant's murderer at the door; Aquinas II-II q.110).
Jack and Lena, scrupulously friends, agree with each other all hour; Hale is appalled ("Somebody fetch a priest; the Catholics are
agreeing"). 🔒 His one unguarded line: **"The commonest lie in the world is not told to murderers at doors. It's told to
policemen, about the dead, by people who loved them."** (His own forty-one seconds; Lena's statement; the Costigans' plea — he knows
only the first.) He looks at the window after it. Sat Oct 3: the Blessing (see Osei, §8.5).
**(E10 plan:)** Wed Oct 14, midterm week, no antagonists' duel: the papers ("That a Man Can Know His Own Heart" / "That He Cannot") are
swapped across the table and the corpses are due Monday. Lena calls Jack "Mr. Costigan"; Hale: "Mr. Costigan has been demoted. How
interesting." On Jeremiah 17:9–10: "The only one in the text who claims to know it is the one I don't believe in. Mr. Costigan, you have
been asked to steal His job." Thu Oct 15, proctoring his PHIL 1 midterm (the essay: the First Way — "state it; then break it"): "Somebody
played a gospel number on the Ferrer carillon at five o'clock this morning. Badly. I was awake; I'm always awake. In eleven years it is the
first thing that tower has done that I've enjoyed. One bell is still flat. Begin." He never learns who.
**(E11 plan:)** Wed Oct 21, the day after Rome announces it will receive Anglicans with their own structure (Oct 20), Hale writes **EXIT · VOICE ·
LOYALTY (HIRSCHMAN, 1970)** and "THE ANGLICANS ARE COMING." on the whiteboard and runs the first refereed duel since E07, **`exit` — "On Leaving"**,
assigned by his E02 rule (*prove the thing you least believe*): **Jack EXIT** ("That a Man Must Leave What He Loves When It Isn't True" — Newman's
*Apologia*, "The Parting of Friends," the conscience toast; Dorothy Day leaving Forster Batterham), **Lena LOYALTY** ("That He Must Stay and Make It Tell
the Truth" — John 6:68, Royce, Ruth 1:16, piety, Hirschman's *voice*). 🔒 **His one unguarded line: "They're bringing the Prayer Book, I'm told.
Cranmer's English. The finest prose ever written by a man who was later burned for it."** He looks at the window. Mid-duel, to Jack, who has been
arguing with the glass: "Mr. Costigan, she's over there." After: **"When you leave something, Mr. Costigan, look at it. Newman did. It's the only
decent thing about leaving."** (🔒 the vicar's son, who left at seventeen.) Under Evan's grade on Jack's midterm (`e10_exam=="defend"`): *He wrote it as
though it were true. Irritating. —A.H.*
**(E11 played:)** the `exit` duel went to **Jack, 5–1** (rigor; no heart) — "argued for leaving like a man who's never been allowed to"; Lena: "It's a
good argument. I hope you never use it." Corridor: "Did you?" — "I've never left anything in my life, Mr. Costigan. I've been thrown out of several."
**(E12, optional, ≤120 words:)** on his dark porch at the cliff end of Del Playa at one in the morning with a glass and Berkeley asleep, as a Spanish
farmer and a Georgia Catholic go by: "Between you, the Counter-Reformation and the Confederacy." Lena: "She'd have eaten you for breakfast,
Professor." "I'd have been honored." No Compline (S2).
**Arc.** S1: the seduction of two minds; the postcard discovery (E14); the dinner party where he's magnificent and then
vile. S2: debates Lena publicly at the Marisol Forum and loses; loves her more for it. S3: writes a book on moral luck
using his students as unnamed examples; Jack finds himself in a footnote. S4: the forty-one seconds come out; disgrace;
leave of absence. S5: sobriety, attempted. S6: cirrhosis. S8: his last lecture is the Philosophy commencement address.
Adulthood: 🔒 dies in 2015; asks for Fr. Osei; what happens in the room is left ambiguous. The show never claims a
deathbed conversion. It lets Jack see Hale's hand move toward his forehead, and stop, or not.

### 8.7 JULIAN PARK-ELLIS
**Full name:** Julian Min-jun Park-Ellis. Born **January 9, 1990** — 19 at the pilot. Oakland (Rockridge). Oakland School
for the Arts '08. Sophomore, Music Composition. Lives off campus in Las Olas (a converted garage behind a surf house on
Del Playa Lane, with a piano he got for free and tuned himself).

**Family.** Mother **Park Soo-jin** ("Susan"), cellist in the Oakland East Bay Symphony. Father **Marcus Ellis**, high-school
chemistry teacher and weekend jazz bassist. Both secular, loving, a little worried about him. An older brother, **Daniel**,
in the Navy.

**Public face.** Quietly handsome; calm; the one you'd trust with anything. Plays guitar and piano, sings at Café Bastille's
Thursday open mic, records songs in his garage onto a Tascam four-track. Secular; gentle; heartbreakingly decent.
Everyone's favorite person to sit next to.

**Private truth. 🔒** He was Nora Calloway's closest friend and her duet partner; they sang at the Point wall at night
freshman year. He loved her; she loved someone she wouldn't name. He wrote a song after she died — "Fresno" — that he plays
once a month and can never finish without his voice going. He doesn't know about the pregnancy or Evan. He suspects
suicide and blames himself for not seeing it.

**Wound.** Nora. Being the friend.

**Want vs. need.** *Want:* to be chosen first by someone. *Need:* to stop accepting second place as proof of goodness.

**Voice.** Soft-spoken, wry, precise; musical metaphors ("that's a suspended chord, it wants to resolve"). Asks real
questions and waits for the answer. Rarely jokes; when he does, it's sly and kind. Says "hella" once a season.
- *"I don't believe any of it. But I believe you do, and I'd like to know what that's like. Is that allowed?"*
- *"Every song I like is in a key that sounds like church. I'm told that's not an accident."*
- *"You don't have to convert me. You just have to let me stand next to you while you do the thing."*

**Look (brief).** Quietly handsome. Medium height, clean-cut, warm brown skin, short dark hair, a calm presence; you'd trust
him with anything. Musician. Wears his father's old cardigans, a Timex, guitar calluses.

**The fatal gap (brief: both).** He respects her faith without ever believing it — he stands lovingly outside the church
door, and no sacramental marriage is possible — *and* he's too easy: gentle and deferential, he never fights her, and she
discovers she needs to be argued with. Perfect on paper and wrong in the bones; the ending is sad. **Hard rule:** they
never sleep together; he never asks her to (it's one of the things that makes him heartbreaking). Theo is openly rude to
him until Jack, who can tell it won't work out, tells Theo to stop (S2E04); Laney is polite and feeds Theo; Margot thinks
he's the healthiest thing in Lena's life and says so.

**Arc (re-planned after S1E11 for the player's exact sequence).** S1: glimpsed on the party stairs; "Fresno" (E04; Lena
leaves in the second chorus, and again before E09). **S1E13, All Souls:** he comes into St. Brendan's after the 5:15 to write
Nora's name in the Book of the Dead "because she would have"; Lena has written it an hour before — their first real
conversation, by the Marian shrine, about Nora (🔒 Nora told him at the Lighthouse, on April 25, that she had a Preview admit
from New Orleans asleep in her room; he has never asked Lena about that night, and she can't bear that he might). That night
Wes ends it. **S1E15:** Café Bastille's Thursday open mic — he plays something new, not "Fresno," and **Lena stays to the end**
for the first time; he walks her home; **together by S1E16** (it is why she can't go to Youngstown). He waits outside St.
Brendan's after the 9 p.m. Mass with two coffees ("I don't go in. I like to be where she comes out" — Theo's "supporter, not
a member" without knowing whose line it is). Thanksgiving apart (Oakland / New Orleans); **New Year's at the Broussards'**
(Mémère calls him *le petit violon*; the Facebook album Jack sees in a Youngstown basement, S2E02). He reads *Confessions* for
her. Theo rude; Laney polite; Margot: "the healthiest thing in her life" (S1E16); Jack tells Theo to stop (S2E04). Red herring
#4 lives in Jack's jealousy (S1E15–S2E05): Julian at the wall at dusk; "Fresno." **He doesn't come to New Orleans** for the
Super Bowl or Mardi Gras when she asks: "It's your city. I'd be a tourist in it. Go." — the gentlest possible refusal and the
whole fatal gap in a line. **The end (S2E11, Sun Feb 21, 2010, the First Sunday of Lent):** she ends it, honestly, within a
week of coming home ("I'd lie for you if you'd let me. You won't let me." — and hers, the true one: "You've never once told me
I was wrong."). Theo does not celebrate in front of him. **Hard rule:** kissing at most. After: a friend; S4 learns what Lena
hid about Nora — the hardest scene he has; **plays at the wedding (SU3)**. Never converts; never stops being decent.

## 10. CHARACTERS THE ROOM INVENTED

### 10.1 WES ARCENEAUX — Lena's boyfriend at the opening 🔒
**Full name:** Wesley Joseph Arceneaux. Born **June 14, 1989** — 20. New Orleans (Gentilly). Jesuit High '07. Junior at
Tulane, civil engineering ("I'm going to build the walls right"). Lena's older brother Luc's best friend — four years
younger, he has followed Luc around since he could walk (the page's wording, S1E07 s08v; "since T-ball" retired after E07).

**Who he is.** During Katrina, at sixteen, Wes took his uncle's aluminum flatboat out on the third day and ran it through
Gentilly and the edge of the Ninth Ward for four days, pulling people off roofs and out of attics. He is a neighborhood
legend. He carried Lena's twin siblings out of the second floor of a neighbor's house. He has been in love with Lena
since she was fifteen, and he waited until she was seventeen to say so. They've dated since her senior-year Mardi Gras
(February 2009). They are "holding the line" together — his idea as much as hers — and they hold it: they never sleep together (hard rule). He's funny (a gentle, goofy, Saints-
obsessed humor), kind, devout in the way of a Jesuit boy: he can do the Ignatian Examen and the Who Dat chant in one
breath. He's the kind of boyfriend parents pray for.

**The unraveling (sad, not expected).** He is not cheating. He is not a cad. **He is losing God.** On the fourth day of the
boat, behind a house on Pauger Street, he heard knocking under a roofline — a woman in an attic — and couldn't get the
axe through the rafters before the water rose and his uncle made him go to the next house; when they came back in the
morning the knocking had stopped. He has never told anyone: not his uncle, not Lena, not a priest. Four years later,
studying the Army Corps of Engineers' failures in a Tulane classroom, he has begun to believe that the water was just
water and the knocking was just a woman and there was no one listening to either of them. He stops going to Mass in
September. He doesn't tell Lena. He drinks a little more each week. He keeps calling her every night at 10 Central / 8
Pacific, sweet and funny, and she keeps not noticing, because she doesn't want to.

**Jack and Wes.** **(E07:)** at dawn on the Delgado roof Lena tells Jack that Wes has bought a ticket — Columbus Day
weekend, "He wants to see the Pacific. I told him it's a threat." **(E08, after the Thérèse night — Lena turns toward him, seen
only through Jack's eyes:)** she calls *him* now, afternoons and late, from the east stairwell (2nd–3rd landing; her laugh carries
up); Thu Oct 1 she mails him a care package at the UCen counter while Jack stands in line behind her — a Mason jar of the Pacific
with sand in the bottom, the lid sealed with electrical tape, **THE PACIFIC. (A PREVIEW.)** in her RELIQUARY capitals, plus a
pound of See's and a Marisol sweatshirt; she argues the clerk out of "liquids" ("It's an ocean, not a liquid"). **Wes is thrilled**:
he calls her at six his time Saturday to say it came ("Luc says it smells like bait. Wes says it smells like me."); Sunday from
the Dome he texts in capitals: "WHO DAT 4-0!!! 5 DAYS. TELL THE OHIO GUY WHO DAT." 🔒 He means every word; the drinking is
still there; nothing about faith reaches the page. Wes visits Marisol Columbus Day weekend (S1E09). Jack expects to hate him and doesn't. They end up on the
beach at 2 a.m. with a bottle of Jameson; Wes tells Jack the attic story — the one he's never told Lena — because Jack is
a stranger, and you can tell a stranger. **(Revised for the timing tragedy:)** Wes tells it in the *past tense*, as a
wound that healed — "I couldn't kneel for a year after. Then she came along and I could" — and shows Jack a photo of
Mémère Odile's ring on his phone: he's going to ask Tony after Lena graduates. 🔒 It's half a lie; he is telling it to
himself. Wes says: "Take care of her. She thinks she's the one doing the carrying." Jack (and the player) come away
believing Wes is solid and the thing is forever; Jack knows something about Lena's boyfriend that Lena doesn't (the attic,
the ring), and it is the beach, the ring and that sentence that make him stop waiting — which is why, by Advent, he has
said yes to Becca. Neither Jack nor the player is shown that it's winding down until E12–E13. *(Re-planned after S1E11: the end
comes on All Souls, S1E13; Becca is made official two days later, S1E14 — see "The end" below.)*
**(E09 plan, after S1E08 — the player's notes for the weekend; supersedes the paragraph above where they differ):** Wes is a
good guy. **Look (new canon):** six-two and easy in it, shoulders from the boat years and Jesuit lacrosse, sunburn that stops
at the sleeve, sandy hair cut short by a barber on Elysian Fields, a front tooth chipped at a lacrosse game, a Saints cap whose
brim he has been bending since 2006; a scuffed black flip phone with a grainy camera; slow Gentilly vowels ("podna," "dawlin',"
"yeah you right"). He flies into SBA Friday afternoon (Tulane's fall break) and out Monday morning; **he sleeps on an air
mattress in 412** (Theo cancelled his motel — hard rule: nobody sleeps with anyone). **He and Jack hit it off at once** — the
Saints' bye, the 1980 bag, the Browns, levees and Lordstown, two sons of men who built things — and **as the weekend goes on he
clearly does not like what he sees between Jack and Lena**: the waltz Jack learned on that beach (path); the tin of Jack's
cigarettes in her pocket, labeled in her hand ("partially consumed by the Reliquarian"); Lena refusing the Point and Jack not
asking why; the roux spoon handed over without a look ("Same as the video" — Laney's Malibu post); two people arguing about the
rich young man as if they were the only two in the kitchen. He never accuses and never sulks; he goes quieter. **The beach (Sun
~2–6 a.m., the Campus Point lifeguard tower, Theo's Jameson):** Theo's question in the car opened it; the attic, told as healed;
**Mémère's ring** — Odile gave it to him at Easter, "for when" — in a grainy photo on the flip phone; he'll ask Tony after she
graduates; "Don't say nothing to her"; **"Take care of her. She thinks she's the one doing the carrying"** — trust and a claim
in one sentence. Monday: a handshake for Jack instead of the hug ("See you, Ohio"); "Take care of yourself, Becca." That night
on the stairwell phone, Lena (overheard): **"He's my friend, Wes."** 🔒 Nothing about Mass or faith reaches the page: he kneels
beside her at the 9:30 (path) and Jack takes it as proof. 🔒 **Jack knows about the ring when Wes ends it (S1E13), and keeps it until the
honesty night in New Orleans (S2E10).** **Becca doesn't notice.** **Tracked from E09** (`wes`: warmth, trust, friction) so Mardi Gras (S2) and the funeral (S5) can
read the weekend.

**(After S1E09 — room canon for the calls; 🔒, never quoted on page beyond Lena's side):** Monday's 8:04 (E09 s13b; Lena's side
overheard — "He's my friend, Wes." / "I know you did." / "Goodnight.") went, on Wes's side: *I like him, dawlin'. I do. … He's in love with
you. You know that, right?* → "He's my friend, Wes." → *I told him to take care of you anyway.* → "I know you did." Tuesday's 8:04 (Jack at
the Anchor; off-page) he tells everything for five minutes — Sully's, the Browns, the beach: "Your Ohio guy told me about his brother.
Prison." and "He drank to it. Where I'm from that's a yes." **From E10 Lena knows that Wes asked Jack to take care of her, that Jack "said
yes" with the bottle, and that Jack told Wes about Danny in one night.** She never learns the ring or the attic. She never repeats "He's in
love with you." Wes left his **green Tulane sweatshirt** with her (she wears it all through E10). Thu Oct 15, 6:02 a.m. PT, he texts her
(ordinary; Obama is in New Orleans that day) — Jack sees only WES on the lit screen. Nothing about faith or drinking reaches the page; the
unraveling stays off until E12–E13.
**(E11 room canon; 🔒, never quoted on page beyond Lena's side):** Sat Oct 24, 8:04 p.m. PT, no cell signal on the Big Sur coast: Lena calls from the
Santa Lucia Motor Court's payphone with Kevin's roll of quarters. Wes picks up (Zimple Street; tired; sweet; he lets her talk). She tells him the monks, the
condors that weren't, "You'd hate the cold," the game tomorrow. **She says "I love you" first and waits; he says it back a half-second late** (on the
page: only the wait). The operator wants seventy-five cents mid-sentence; she has the rest of the roll and doesn't feed it. Sunday, **Tony tells Jack on
speaker "Wes says y'all got a Browns fan out there"** (Wes has talked about "the Ohio boy" at the Broussards' table, kindly; Luc and Tony know Jack
exists). Sun 8:04 p.m. PT (10:04 CT) he calls; she's at the Delgado stove; in the hall: "Did you see it? … We came back." … "Wes?" … "No. It's okay. Go to
sleep." (🔒 he'd been drinking since the fourth quarter at a friend's — never on the page; the unraveling stays off until E12–E13.)
**(E12 room canon; 🔒, never quoted on page beyond Lena's side):** Sat Oct 31, 8:04 p.m. PT (10:04 CT), **Frenchmen Street** on Halloween —
Voodoo Fest weekend in City Park, a brass band in the street behind him; this time he says "I love you" first and she answers at once; "Who dat,
dawlin'" / "Who dat, baby."; he thinks Flannery O'Connor is somebody from the Saintsations until she explains the peacocks. He has been drinking since
noon (never on the page). **The last ordinary call.** Sunday morning he is at St. Louis No. 3 with a bucket and a brush, as every year since 2005,
when Odile asks about the ring (E13).

**The end (re-planned after S1E11 for the player's exact sequence; supersedes S1E21): S1E13, Mon Nov 2, 2009 — All Souls.**
*Sun Nov 1, All Saints, New Orleans (off-page; reported to Lena by text and to Jack by her face):* the Broussards and the Guidrys
whitewash the family tombs at **St. Louis Cemetery No. 3 on Esplanade** (Grandmère Lucille is there), as New Orleans families do
on All Saints; Wes comes, as he has every year since 2005, with a bucket and a brush. In front of everyone **Mémère Odile asks
him, "Et la bague, Wesley? Quand?"** — the ring. Josie texts Lena in capitals (MEMERE ASKED WES ABOUT THE RING!!!). Lena now knows.
*Mon Nov 2:* the Saints beat Atlanta on Monday night (35–27, 7–0); no WHO DAT text. At 9:30 Pacific Lena calls him from the east
stairwell and asks the one question she has decided not to ask since Labor Day: when did you last go to Mass. **He tells her the
truth** — September; the attic on Pauger Street and the knocking that stopped (the first time she has heard it); the drinking, a
little; the ring in his desk drawer on Zimple Street, which Odile gave him at Easter "for when." **"You'd do it. You'd marry me
anyway. That's why I can't let you."** He will not let her marry a man who stands at the back of the church at their children's
baptisms with his hands in his pockets. She argues — she argued LOYALTY in Hale's seminar twelve days ago and won — and she loses,
because you cannot argue someone into faith, and she knows it, and that is the worst thing she learns all year. (E11's Dorothy Day
line — "She went toward something, and he wouldn't come" — is the shape, inverted: he is the one who goes.) **Jack sits on the
stairs with her until dawn and does not kiss her.** He is two days from being Becca's official boyfriend, and she is the one who
sends him back: **"You have a girl, Jack. A nice one. Don't you dare."** 🔒 **Jack already knows the attic and the ring** (E09). Default:
he says nothing on the stairs, and the ring is the first thing he tells her on the honesty night in New Orleans (S2E10). Path: if
the player tells her now ("He told me. At the tower."), she goes formal and polite for a full minute — "You knew there was a ring and
you let me find out from a thirteen-year-old" — then lets him stay anyway (trust −, friction +; S2E10's first confession becomes
the reason he stayed away, which she has never heard). 🔒 **The ring goes back to Odile** in its box, by Wes's own hand, after Mass the
following Sunday (reported by Josie, S2E08).

**After.** Lena keeps praying for him. He is at the Broussards' Thanksgiving table as he has been since 2005 (Luc's best friend;
off-page; it is one reason Jack's invitation — "Come where nobody knows his name" — is kind). **Wes at the Saints' victory parade
and Mardi Gras 2010 (S2E08–E10)** — sober-ish, kind, broken; he and Jack meet again ("See you, Ohio," paid back on the neutral
ground); on Lundi Gras, on the levee, to Jack only: **"Don't wait on nobody, podna. I waited on God four years."** 🔒 **He dies on Aug
29, 2011 (SU2 finale)**, the sixth anniversary of the storm, of an accidental overdose of oxycodone and alcohol. Lena calls Jack
at 3 a.m. for the first time since the rupture (S4E22); Jack goes to New Orleans for the funeral (S5E01). It is the hinge of the
reconciliation. *(Death register: reconsider before SU2 based on the path; the Showrunner may instead let Wes live and return to
the Church in adulthood.)* **Mémère's ring** (🔒 room's call, S5E18): Odile, ninety, may offer it to Jack when he asks Tony's
blessing at Thanksgiving 2011 — "It was always for her. It only needed the right hands." Jack may take it, or buy his own with RA
money; the player chooses, and Lena's reaction is the point either way (registry #78).

**Voice.** Easy, slow, goofy, Gentilly-Y'at ("where y'at," "dawlin'," "yeah you right"). Talks about the Saints like
scripture. Underneath, long pauses.
- *"You're the Ohio guy. She said you were a jagoff. That means she likes you, I've known her since she had a retainer."*

### 10.2 SOL ARRIAGADA HOLM — Jack's magnetic girl 🔒
*(Rebuilt to the player's design, brief "Jack's two girls": from Theo's LA orbit; the fire without the faith.)*
**Full name:** Soledad Arriagada Holm. Born **September 11, 1989** (the 16th anniversary of the Chilean coup; her mother
calls her "my counter-revolution"). Raised in Los Feliz, Los Angeles. **A director's daughter and a former actress:** her
father, **Lars Holm**, a Swedish documentary cameraman turned studio director, has made three prestige thrillers for
Meridian Pictures (Richard Vance produced two). Sol played the middle daughter on a fictional cable family drama,
*Harbor Lights* (2001–05), from eleven to fifteen, and at seventeen starred in her father's one indie, which Meridian
distributed and which got her a Spirit nomination she didn't go to collect. She quit acting at eighteen. NYU Tisch film,
Fall 2008 – Fall 2009 (she leaves after three semesters: "New York was a very expensive way to be cold"); **transfers to
Marisol in Film and Media for Spring 2010 (S2)**; a falling-down Las Olas house on Del Playa with two surfers and a darkroom
(she drives up to see it, and Theo, on Fri Dec 11, 2009 — S1E21). Drives a 1987 Volvo 240 wagon with two boards on the roof.

**Theo knows her.** Since they were twelve: Meridian holiday parties, premieres where they were each other's date, a
summer at fifteen they never discuss (nothing happened; that's the point). She calls him "Teddy" only to needle him; she is
the one person besides Richard who can. Theo knows exactly what she is — *"She collects saints, Costigan. She takes them
apart to see how they work, and she's very kind about it."* — and stays, as always, Team Lena.

**Who she is.** Fierce, intelligent, funny, sexy and utterly compelling. Surfs at dawn at the Point break; shoots Super 8
and 16mm; reads Neruda, Bataille, Duras, and Simone Weil (the last one secretly, and that's her crack). Sexually frank and
unashamed — not a party girl, an adult in a room of children. **Respectful of Jack's faith and mildly curious, but not
really interested in it**: she'll come to the 9 p.m. Mass once "for the light," and mean it about the light. Her flaws sit
exactly where Becca's strengths are: she won't try with his faith, won't go to Mass for him, won't pretend. Her mother,
**Isidora Arriagada**, a poet, was detained at Villa Grimaldi in 1975; the Catholic Church's Vicaría de la Solidaridad
got her out and to Sweden, where she met Lars. So Sol cannot dismiss the Church, and that maddens her.

**What she wants from Jack.** Precisely the thing he won't give her, and to understand why he won't. She is not trying to
corrupt him; she is trying to find out if he's real. She is the pull of the world at its most honest and beautiful.
**Hard rule:** they never sleep together. The arc lives at the line — kissing, making out, her darkroom, his resolve
cratering — and it is her incomprehension of the line, and his inability to make her see it, that ends them.

**The friends.** Laney is openly rude to her, like all the others, and privately, begrudgingly likes her (they smoke on
the Pi Lam roof and never mention it). **Lena can't stand her** — and doesn't tell Laney to be nice. Theo is polite and
Team Lena. Margot finds her "the only interesting person Costigan has ever dated, which is an indictment."

**Look.** Tall, long black hair usually wet from the ocean, sun-dark skin, a gap between her front teeth, a man's
steel watch (her father's), a Bolex camera, a wetsuit peeled to the waist, paint under her nails. A single silver earring.
Wears LA money like Theo does, as if it were cheap.

**Voice.** Spanish-inflected LA; switches to Spanish when she means it; direct to the point of rudeness; laughs at her own
jokes before she finishes them; questions as dares.
- *"You want me. I want you. Explain to me, slowly, with diagrams, why we're standing in this kitchen."*
- *"Your God got my mother out of a cellar. I'm not ungrateful. I'm just not his."*

**Arc (re-planned after S1E11 for the player's exact sequence: moved from S3 to Dec 2009 – Feb 2010).** **S1E12 glimpse:** up
from LA for Theo's Halloween party with his LA crowd, filming the chaos with the Bolex; Theo, seeing her: "Soledad. Don't." Jack
sees a tall girl with a camera; they don't meet (she films him, in costume, without his knowing — **on the played path he is St. Isidore in Becca's straw
hat**, so E21's "You were the one in the hat" holds exactly; 🔒 she collects saints, and he came dressed as one; she films him on the staircase
during the costume parade; Theo's "Soledad. Don't." comes from the gallery, not loud; she lowers the camera, smiling; no names exchanged; no
journal card). **S1E21 (Fri Dec 11 – Sat Dec
12): they meet.** She's up to see her Del Playa house and crashes in 412 on the Aerobed bought for Wes; Jack comes in from the
Anchor at 1:40 a.m. and finds her on the fire stair with *Gravity and Grace* and one of Margot's contraband Djarums; she asks whose
crucifix that is ("Good. I was afraid it was Teddy's."); "You were the one in the hat. I have you on film."; they argue till the
marine layer goes gray — the first person since Lena who'll fight him at 3 a.m., and the first who doesn't care whether he wins.
Theo, awake in the dark: "Soledad." — "I know, Teddy." She walks him to the 5 a.m. Guadalupe *Mañanitas* "for the light," stops at
the door, and leaves; Lena, coming up the steps with Julian and two coffees, sees. **S1E22:** she films the Ferrer Tower's "Hotel
California" on the last day of finals, beside Jack, while Lena watches from across the Commons: "I'll be back in January. I have a
darkroom now. Write me." **Winter break (S2E01–E02):** Facebook messages and texts — the RAZR at the Glenwood Avenue kitchen table,
the family Dell in the basement — Christmas Eve (the Senate vote; the Pope knocked down at St. Peter's), the underwear bomber, New
Year's at midnight Pacific / three a.m. Ohio. **S2E03 (Jan 2010): they start dating** — dawn surf at the Point break (she teaches
him; he is bad at it), her darkroom, the 9 p.m. Mass once "for the light," and meant. **Lena hates every minute.** **Proxy fight #1**
(S2E05) and **#2** (S2E09), §24.6. **The darkroom near-miss (S2E06):** it stops short — she stops it, when she sees he's going to hate
himself in the morning ("I don't sleep with men who'll confess me on Saturday"), or he does; resolve decides *how*, never
*whether*. **Berlin (S2E06–E10):** her father's film is in competition at the Berlinale (Feb 11–21, 2010; the film is fictional);
she asks Jack to come; he goes to New Orleans instead, which is its own answer. **The end (S2E11, Tue Feb 23, 2010, her darkroom):**
Jack ends it, honestly, within a week of New Orleans — "I'm in love with someone else, and I won't make you a story I'm telling
myself." Sol: "I know who she is, Jack. Everybody knows who she is. Explain to me, slowly, why it took you a parade." Four days
later the Chile earthquake (Sat Feb 27, magnitude 8.8) hits her mother's family in Concepción; Jack calls her and stays on the
phone an hour; Lena knows, and says nothing. **After:** she stays at Marisol (Film and Media) — a friend; Laney's secret roof-smoking
partner; possibly Theo's director of photography on the Lordstown documentary (S6–S7, room's call); she returns once more in
adulthood as a friend. Never a villain; she was right about how afraid Jack is. **Lena brings her up for the rest of their lives**
as a running joke, the cheapest fallback argument she owns ("Maybe Soledad would agree with you, Costigan." — registry #80).

### 10.3 PAIGE SATO-WHITMAN — RETIRED (after S1E06; folded into Becca, §10.4)
*(The tryer passes to Becca Lindell. The player met Becca on the page in E02 and E06 and responded to her as exactly the
tryer's blend — "sweet, faithful and without fire." Paige never reached the page. Her best pieces move to Becca: Coffee &
Donuts and knowing everyone's order; the color-coded Aquinas; the Five Ways learned by heart; the question that makes Osei
laugh; the hermit crab at the Blessing of the Animals; and a grandmother's rosary as a quiet mirror to Lena's never-asked
question (Becca's is the Sunshine Mine, not Manzanar). Do not use the name Paige Sato-Whitman for anyone.)*

### 10.4 BECCA LINDELL — Jack's "tryer" 🔒 (promoted after S1E06)
*(Brief, "Jack's two girls": the tryer, "the girl you bring home to mama"; a sincere, shallow cradle Catholic met at Newman,
the nicest girl there; the faith and the effort without the fire. **She is the first of Jack's two girls, in the
timing-tragedy slot.** Player note after S1E06: the player *assumes* Becca is the tryer and calls her "the exact blend we
were looking for of sweet, faithful and without fire." **Never confirm or deny it to the player; keep exactly that blend.**)*
**Full name:** Rebecca Anne Lindell. Born **May 31, 1991** (the Visitation) — 18. A cattle-and-hay ranch on the Rathdrum
road outside Coeur d'Alene, Idaho ("it's a lake, people think it's a cheese"). Homeschooled through twelfth grade (a parish
co-op at St. Pius X; 4-H; the county fair). Freshman, Biology, pre-vet ("large animal. Cows don't lie to you"). Portolá
Hall; her roommate is a nursing major from Spokane. Drives her brother Eli's red 1994 Ford Ranger with Idaho plates and a
Rathdrum feed-store sticker (she and her father drove it down in August).
**Family.** Father **Karl Lindell**, rancher, Knights of Columbus, says grace like he's reading a contract; mother **Marie**,
who ran the house and the homeschool and lights candles for everything (E06: "She's lighting a candle in Coeur d'Alene").
Seven siblings — five brothers, two sisters; ten at dinner; no TV; the oldest sister is the one you'd be scared of; Eli lost
a front tooth to a Benedictine, Father Anselm, at a church-picnic volleyball game. 🔒 **The rosary** she prays every night,
wrapped twice around her wrist at Mass, was her grandmother's — **Agnes Lindell**, whose husband Walt died in the **Sunshine
Mine fire at Kellogg, Idaho (May 2, 1972; ninety-one men)**. Agnes prayed it at the mine gate for the eight days it took,
and on the eighth day they brought two men up alive, and neither one was Walt. Becca tells this, when she tells it (S2E03),
as a sweet fact about a rosary; she has never asked her grandmother one question about those eight days. (A quiet mirror:
Lena has never asked her mother about Memorial; Nonno's Black Monday is a candle on Glenwood Avenue. Becca's not-asking is
sweetness; Lena's is a wound. Jack sees both, and the narration never says so.)
**Who she is.** Cute, sweet, warm, frank, funny in a dry, delighted way, much tougher than she looks — pulled a calf at
twelve, can butcher a deer, whistles a herd off a county road with no hands. Wants to be married by twenty-one and says so on
the second date; wants seven or nine children ("Odd numbers, so there's a tiebreaker"). Everyone at Newman thinks she and
Jack are perfect together; Mrs. Fitch, Tyler and the Coffee & Donuts table say "you two" by October. **A sincere, shallow
cradle Catholic:** Mass every Sunday of her life, the Glory Be when a cow is kicking her, her grandmother's rosary, Newman
like a family — and she has never wrestled with any of it. "I don't need it to be complicated. I need it to be true. It's
true. So."
**How she tries** (with his faith *and* his rigor): asks him to teach her the Five Ways and learns them by heart on a set of
bleachers, delighted and untroubled (E07); reads Aquinas with color-coded tabs from her clipboard; reads *Caritas in
Veritate* because he quoted it on a beach; comes to Hale's public lecture in her nicest cardigan; reads O'Connor and hates it
("Why is everybody so *mean*?"), reads Percy and loves it; asks Osei a question so good he laughs for a full minute (she has
no idea why it was good). Jack's arguments delight her and slide off. Asked if she ever thinks it might not be true: "No," —
honestly puzzled — "Do you?" She respects his line and does not test it; she is waiting too.
**Hard rule:** they never sleep together. Kissing, warmth, her head on his shoulder at the 9 p.m. Mass — and nothing deeper
there. That is the whole tragedy. **Never a placeholder** (brief): Jack already knows how he feels about Lena, so Becca must
have real gravitational weight of her own — she is the whole good life everyone wants for him (his mother, sight unseen:
"Seven! Catholic?"), and on some nights he wants it too; the player must be able to love her.
**The friends (brief).** **Laney hates her on principle** and is openly rude ("Is she Amish or just punctual?" — E07);
**Lena tells Laney to be nice — and means it, which is worse**; **Theo is flawlessly polite to her face and texts Laney her
schedule** (always Team Lena). Margot can't find anything to object to and finds that suspicious. Kevin likes her and tells
Theo and Laney to leave it alone.
**Look.** Five-seven, strong, sun-browned, a dark-blond braid as thick as a bell rope, sunburn along the part, freckles she
doesn't cover, forearms that have done things to hay, marker on her knuckles from lettering posters; Wranglers, Ropers, a
Carhartt vest, a spiral planner and the clipboard. Her scar story about anybody is the straight one.
**Voice.** Frank, unembarrassed, practical; ranch competence as comedy; scientific about feelings; never sarcastic; never a
joke at her faith.
- *"I've pulled a calf. Give it here."* · *"You can think about it. I'm patient. We had a whole talk."* · *"Glory Be. You can
  get through it while a cow's kicking you."*
**Arc.** E02 "Put me down" (the BBQ poster). **E06 date #1, retroactively** (the cleanup; on the played path Jack told her it
was "a beach cleanup *and* a date"; FRIDAY? on the clipboard; the Franciscan scar telling). **E07 date #2** (Fri Sep 25):
Marisol High's Friday-night football game in the Ranger; the First Way on the bleachers; "Do you ever think it might not be
true?"; a kiss on the cheek "so there's no confusion"; Laney's first rudeness in the Fishbowl. **E08 (revised after S1E07 for the
player's own two-hander design): date #3 is Jack's** — out of the guilt of the Thérèse night he plans it for the very next day,
with real effort, and **he asks and he pays** (Thu Oct 1 call from the east stairwell; Fri Oct 2): by path, **Nonna's Friday
sauce** cooked in the Newman House kitchen (Becca keeps Friday abstinence year-round "because my family does"; Nonna, on the
phone, remembers *venerdì* perfectly and asks about the girl twice), **the elephant seals at Piedras Blancas at sunset** in
Kevin's borrowed Odyssey (Lena's 3–0 in white polish on the back glass there and back), or **two rented horses on the dunes**
(a week of tips; Becca rides like she was born on one). **Becca is thrilled** on every path ("Nobody's ever planned a thing for
me. I'm the one who plans the thing."). She has prayed for "two in the morning" every night since the bleachers and says so (on
`e7_doubt=="twoam"`) — the night after he spent two in the morning on a beach with Lena. First seed of E10's crack, without
cruelty: "You're always somewhere else a little. That's okay. My dad's like that in calving season." **Sat Oct 3, the Blessing
of the Animals,** is a Newman event, not a date: she brings a hermit crab borrowed from the Marine Lab touch tank in a Tupperware,
named **Thomas** ("Like your First Way") — Laney: "Who brings a *crab*."; she asks Osei whether a borrowed crab's blessing is hers
or the state of California's, and he laughs for a full minute; Mrs. Fitch says "you two" in Lena's hearing. **E10 the first crack:** "You look at me like I'm homework." — and instead of ending it, she
stays: "Then do your homework, Jack. I'll wait." (The tryer tries; it makes it worse later.) **(Re-planned after S1E11 for the player's exact sequence.)** **E12** Halloween in the couples costume he handed in early
(`e11_costume`); cheerfully confused by the girl dressed as Flannery O'Connor ("Who's she?"); home by 11:40 for the 6 a.m. tank
("Farmer."). **E14 (Wed Nov 4): official** — "My mom's been calling you my boyfriend since September. Can I tell her she's
right?" — **two days after Wes ends it with Lena**: the tragedy of timing at its sharpest (Lena free for the first time since the
porch, and she has sent him back; and Jack will not make Becca the girl he left the minute someone else was free). The Newman
table cheers; Theo is polite; Laney is not; Lena is lovely to Becca, which is unbearable. Coffee & Donuts, his order remembered.
**E16** the *New Moon* night (Becca loves it sincerely; Laney rude on principle; Lena, in the row behind — beside Julian — tells
Laney to be nice). **E17** she drives Jack and Theo to SBA in the Ranger before her own flight to Spokane: **"Next time, I want to
meet your nonna."** **E18** (Youngstown; she is off-page): Maria — "Next time you bring Idaho. Christmas!" — and Jack understands
that bringing her into that house would be a lie bordering on cruel. **E19 (Wed Dec 2, the first week of Advent): Jack ends it**,
kindly and honestly, in the Ranger outside Portolá with the heater ticking: he can't take her home; it would be a lie, and she'd
believe it. She sees — without cruelty, because she is never cruel — that he has been loving her a little in order not to love
someone else, and names it: *"I did everything right. I know I did. It just isn't the thing, is it."* He has no argument. Nobody is
wrong. **After:** a friend, carefully; Coffee & Donuts still knows his order; the Sunshine Mine rosary is told as a sweet fact when
they finally talk as friends (S2E16, room's call — never before); she marries in the summer of 2011, at twenty — by twenty-one, as
she said — a Veritas missionary (room's call: Tyler Brandt is available), which Margot considers a hate crime; **she comes to Jack
and Lena's wedding (July 21, 2012)**, pregnant with the first of nine, and cries at the vows.

**(E09 plan, after S1E08 — the player: "Becca must outlast Wes… more Jack and Becca now"):** **New canon:** a work-study job at
the Marine Lab touch tank (Thursday afternoons; Friday 6–8 a.m. — feeding, salinity, the pump), which is how she could sign
Thomas out. **Her eyes are gray** (Jack has never noticed; Laney makes him find out). At the Blessing she asked Jack for his home
address "for my Christmas cards — I do them in October" and used it early: **a birthday card for Rosie with a drawing of Thomas
and six true facts about hermit crabs** (Gina: "ma cried. not really. kind of"). Friday dawn she takes Jack to see Thomas home;
the Nobel on KCBX ("Good for him! Maybe it's in advance — like a ribbon at the fair before the calf's done anything, so it'll walk
right" — the deep thing said by accident, delighted, with no idea why it's good). She's thrilled to meet Wes and has prayed for
him and Lena since September; she and Wes, the two good ones, take to each other at once. **She doesn't notice and she trusts Jack
wholeheartedly — both** (brief): "I love that he has somebody to argue with. I'm no good at it." / "Jack's the most honest person I
know." Laney rude ("She labeled the marshmallows"); Lena "Laney. Be nice." (and means it); Theo flawless (he lets her drive his
grandfather's SL to the Madonna Inn; she drives stick; he is moved against his will). First seed of "homework" (E10): at the
Newlywed Game, cheerfully, "He studies me like a test and still fails." **E10** (brief, after E08): Jack tells Lena that Becca had
him explain the First Way at a football game — "Aquinas and football, that's the dream girl right there" — and doesn't hear himself
describe Lena; then: it was empty. From there Jack knows he shouldn't be with her, and stays (E09's ring is why). **Becca outlasts
Wes** (Wes ends E13; Becca E19) — never a placeholder; her goodness is the knife, never the joke.
**(E10 plan, after S1E09:)** Wed night, the eighth floor of the Brick: a gallon Ziploc labeled **MIDTERM SURVIVAL — JACK** (granola bars, a
clementine, earplugs, a Glory Be card, *You'll do great! I prayed. —B*) and a clementine for Lena out of her own pocket ("Hi, Lena! Good
luck!"); asleep by ten ("Farmer."). **She prays for two in the morning "like always"** — her 6:41 a.m. text, read on the eighth floor with
Lena asleep on his shoulder. Thu 12:10 (fork): "You look *exhausted*. Were you at the library all night?" "Till seven." (true). Fri Oct 16,
***Where the Wild Things Are*** opening night at **the Rialto on Mission** (new: a 1930s single screen cut into four in 1987): she read the
book to her brothers a hundred times; her favorite line is the last one, *and it was still hot* (never glossed). **Maria wrote her a
thank-you note** for Rosie's crab on St. Brigid's stationery (the card's return address). Her **"Not yet"** joke (`e9_poem=="knee"` — her
mother asked if she said yes) isn't one. Her clipboard page **WE SHOULD MATCH!!** — St. Isidore & St. Maria de la Cabeza ("the only married
couple who are both saints" — Jack: Joachim and Anne), Max & a Wild Thing ("I have ears from 4-H"), ~~a cowboy & a cow~~, *ask Jack*. He reads
it like a problem set. **"You look at me like I'm homework."** She doesn't end it: **"Then do your homework, Jack. I'll wait."** She tears off
the page: "**Due Halloween.**" (E11: Jack hands it in six days early — `e11_costume`; E12 pays it on every path, §22 E12.) After the PHIL 1 midterm's First Way question Jack knows
he described Lena when he described "the dream girl"; from here he knows he shouldn't be with Becca, and stays (the ring is why).
**(E11 plan, after S1E10 — the player: both relationships now on borrowed time; Jack pushes through anyway because of the ring):** Thu Oct 22 at the
12:10 she has saved him a seat and this time he's in it (`e10_noon=="fishbowl"`: "You came!"); [e10_homework=="not"] "You said I'm not homework. So I'm not
asking about the list." Invited to Big Sur (path), **she can't**: the Marine Lab's weekend volunteer has the swine flu and the tech is at a conference in
Monterey, so she's on the touch tank both mornings — "Thomas needs me." **"Bring me a fruitcake. The monks make one. Grandma Agnes orders it every
Christmas — she says it's the only honest fruitcake in America."** (🔒 Agnes is the Sunshine Mine grandmother; S2E03 tells it; here she is only a
fruitcake.) Three texts while he's out of range (*fed thomas!! have fun!! see a condor for me* · *praying for u guys on the road :) goodnight jack* · *is it
beautiful??*); a condor's wing-tag number texted back (path) — she looks it up. **Sun Oct 25, Portolá steps, 6:30 p.m.: the fruitcake ("It's
*honest*!") and the homework, six days early — `e11_costume`:** `isidore` (St. Isidore & St. Maria de la Cabeza — her first; Lena's advice taken),
`wildthing` (Max & a Wild Thing — her 4-H lamb ears), or `moviegoer` (Jack's own: Binx Bolling & Kate Cutrer; he'll find her her own copy at Driftwood —
he never lends his). "*Early!* Nobody's early but me." She does everything right again. Laney's rudeness on principle is by text this week; Lena: "Laney.
Be nice." Jack walks home knowing, and stays.
**(E11 played:)** invited (`e11_becca_invite=="ask"`) — "Thomas needs me"; the honest fruitcake; **the homework: "Isidore and Maria. Your first one."**
(`e11_costume=="isidore"`) — "Farmers! … *Early!* Nobody's early but me." St. Isidore has been on the Ranger's visor since August (E07 canon): she
wanted this one all along.
**(E12 plan, the played path:)** she **sews Jack's costume** on the Newman machine — a tunic from a **Rathdrum Feed & Seed** sack, a rope belt, her own
straw hat (Osei lends the hoe); she is **St. Maria de la Cabeza** in a white lace mantilla from **Mrs. Fitch's basket**, with a clay jug (Maria crossed
the flooded Jarama on her shawl — "I can't do that part"); crestfallen for one second when Osei says Maria was beatified, not canonized: "She's a
saint to *me*." At the door: a halo, obviously. **"Who's she?"** — of Lena as Flannery O'Connor ("Is she a saint?" "Not yet, hon." "Then she's a
*sinner*! That's the *theme*!"). Cheerfully confused by "American Gothic" all night. When the crowd chants it at Jack and Lena on the staircase,
**Lena takes Becca's hand and puts her on the stairs beside Jack — "That's his Maria. I'm just a novelist who wandered in."** (or Jack does it himself,
path) — and the crowd, charmed by a sincere ranch girl with a water jug, makes her **Best Saint** (a gold-sprayed halo): **"I've never won anything
that wasn't a heifer!"** Lena applauds hardest. Home at 11:40 for the 6 a.m. tank — **"Farmer."** — a goodnight kiss at the foot of the staircase in
front of the Georgia novelist; [walk] at the Ranger on Ocean Road: "Tomorrow, the 9:30? I'll save you a seat." Sunday: the seat saved; the gold halo
shown to Mrs. Fitch — **"You two!"** in Lena's hearing; her Facebook comment under Laney's *american gothic???* photo: "we were St. Isidore and St.
Maria!! :) BEST SAINT!!!" She knows nothing of the street, the Point, the duel or the hat. Her goodness is the knife.

### 10.5 PRIYA RAMAN — the junior RA
**Born October 14, 1988.** Fremont, California. Junior, Biochemistry. Delgado Hall 4th-floor RA (Room 401). Three jobs
(RA, library circulation, organic chem tutor). Overwhelmed; laminated everything; "per my email"; color-coded door tags;
an air horn for emergencies. Parents run a motel in Fremont and believe she's pre-med. 🔒 She's quietly failing organic
chemistry and has changed her major on paper without telling them.

🔒 **Nora connection:** Priya was Nora Calloway's freshman roommate (Delgado 2007–08, Room 222) and her best friend that
year. They drifted sophomore year (Nora moved to Serra Hall with Sloane Kittredge, got secretive). Priya has a box of Nora's
things in the RA storage closet — the Calloways never collected it — including Nora's journal, which Priya has never been
able to open past the first page. Her rule "Nobody goes to the Point after dark" is personal. On Nora's birthday she buys two mylar balloons at the Safeway
(two for one), ties one to the cross before her 8 a.m. orgo lab, in daylight, and keeps the other in the 4th-floor lounge
with a grocery cupcake. Facebook keeps suggesting Nora to her residents (no memorial status exists until late Oct 2009); she
has reported it three times. She knows the Memorare by heart because Nora prayed it in the shower every morning (Priya is
Hindu).

**Voice.** Rapid, bureaucratic, accidentally hilarious, passive-aggressive in Comic Sans. "Per the Residential Life
Handbook." "I'm going to need you to not." When she breaks, she breaks completely, then re-laminates.
- *"Rule one: nobody goes to the Point after dark. That's not a Res Life rule. That's my rule. I will know."*

**Arc.** S1: comic relief who becomes a source (she tells Jack Nora's name, E04; shows him the box, E11). S2: opens the
journal. Later seasons: switches to public health, tells her parents (Diwali episode, S5).
**(E10 plan — the box moves up from E11:)** Tuesday's storm floods the **RA storage closet in the Delgado basement** (beside the laundry; RA
STORAGE — NOT A STUDY ROOM). At 1:40 a.m. Wednesday, studying for her 8 a.m. orgo midterm in the warm laundry room, Priya unlocks it and Jack
helps save the boxes: **CALLOWAY · 222 · HOLD FOR FAMILY**, HOLD underlined twice, the bottom corner soaked. Inside: a Selma High sweatshirt,
CDs, a mug, a photo of two girls laughing in a dorm window (one is Priya), and a black-and-white composition book — **Nora's journal**. She
opens the cover only: *Nora Calloway · Delgado 222 · If found, please return. Reward: I'll sing you something.* She has never gotten past it.
She breaks ("I *tutor* organic chemistry. I'm *failing* organic chemistry.") and re-laminates (BASEMENT FLOODED · DRYERS 1–3 ONLY). **The box
lives in 401 from now on.** Wednesday night she works the Brick's overnight circulation desk; near dawn she "didn't see" Jack and Lena ("The
scanner's been broken since two"). Saturday is Diwali: a battery diya on top of the box (HAPPY DIWALI · ELECTRIC DIYAS ONLY · FIRE CODE). 🔒
Nothing in the box points at Hale, Evan, the jacket or Lena; the April 23 entry waits for S2.

### 10.6 RAFI OCHOA — the floor dealer
**Rafael Ignacio Ochoa.** Born **June 2, 1991**. Marisol townie (the Westside, near the harbor). Freshman, Environmental
Studies, on a Cal Grant and a surf-team spot. Delgado 204 (his roommate never showed; "the ghost of 204"). Discreet weed and
Adderall dealer: a tackle box of "vitamins," delivery by skateboard, calls everyone "boss," quotes his abuela and Marcus
Aurelius (his abuela gave him the *Meditations*). Mellow, polite, scrupulously honest about prices. 🔒 **His father is
Detective Sergeant Ray Ochoa of the Marisol Police Department**, who investigated and closed Nora Calloway's death as an
accident. 🔒 Rafi, then a high-school senior, sold Nora two Xanax bars at the Lighthouse party on April 25, 2009. He hasn't
sold bars since; he says, on Jack's first night, "Not bars, though. I don't do bars anymore." He doesn't know whether the
pills mattered. He's terrified that they did.
**(E06 plan, played path:)** Jack (living route in the pilot) never heard "Not bars" or saw the tackle box; E06 re-plants
both after his father's stop on Ocean Road: *"Weed. Adderall around midterms. Not bars, though. I don't do bars anymore."*
(🔒 Nora's two bars; no gloss.) Rafi skates; La Flaca was "reported abandoned at the harbor in 2007" (Ray's line, true).

**Voice.** Surfer-mellow, courtly, philosophical. "Boss." "No stress." "My abuela says the devil's patient, so be faster."
**Arc.** S1: supply and comedy; offers Jack money-for-errands during the money crisis (E15). S2: his father's case file
becomes a target. S4: his part comes out; his father's reaction is the best scene Ray Ochoa ever gets.

### 10.7 EVAN MARSH — the TA with secrets 🔒
**Born February 11, 1980.** Duluth, Minnesota. PhD candidate in Philosophy (dissertation on Kierkegaard and moral
injury, Hale advising, year seven, stalled). TA for PHIL 1's Thursday 4 p.m. section (Hollis 108) — Jack and Lena's
section. Lapsed Lutheran. Dry, exhausted, precise, kind to struggling students, savage to lazy ones.

**The secret.** He was Nora Calloway's boyfriend from October 2008 to her death. She was in his section; they told no one
(it would have cost him his funding and her her reputation). She was eight weeks pregnant. On April 25 they fought by
text; at 12:05 a.m. she texted him from the Point: *"I'm at the Point. Please come. I'm keeping it. I want to keep it. I
want you to want it."* He read it at 12:20 and drove. At 12:31 his headlights caught a tall blond kid running from the
Point path toward Seacliff Road clutching something dark. He went to the wall, saw nothing, called her phone, heard
nothing, assumed she'd left to punish him, and drove home furious. At dawn surfers found her. He told police he'd been
asleep and seen her text in the morning; he deleted it. He has spent four months hunting for a tall blond boy.

**What he does.** He is not a villain; he is a coward that night who is now trying to be brave in the wrong way. He sees
Theo at the Halloween chaos (S1E12) and goes cold. He begins asking careful questions (S1E19: "Your roommate — was he at
Preview Weekend last April?"). In S2 he becomes dangerous — to Theo, and to himself.

**Look.** Lanky, prematurely gray at the temples, wire glasses, a stained Carhartt jacket, a bike he rides everywhere
because he sold his car in the summer (🔒 he couldn't drive past the Point). Smokes American Spirits.
**Voice.** Minnesota-flat, dry, exact; Kierkegaard quotes; long silences. *"Purity of heart is to will one thing. I've
never managed two."*

**Arc.** S1: the man at the end of the Lighthouse driveway (pilot); the section; the questions. S2: the anniversary
confrontation. S3: dates Margot; she finds the text history he never deleted from his laptop. S4: tells the truth to the
police and to the Calloways — the pregnancy, the text, the blond boy. Clears Nora of the suicide whisper. Loses his PhD.
Later: teaches philosophy at a prison education program in Minnesota; writes Jack once a year.
**(E11 plan, 🔒:)** Thu Oct 22, section: he returns the PHIL 1 midterms (Jack's: "A–. Defended, not broken. —E.M."), then reads Kierkegaard aloud —
*Works of Love*, "The Work of Love in Recollecting One Who Is Dead" (the dead can't repay you; it is the freest love) — and his voice goes on the second
paragraph; he lets section out at 4:31 without a word. Monday Oct 26 is six months. Lena is very still. Nobody on the page knows why.
**(E12 plan, 🔒:)** Sat Oct 31, ~11:55 p.m., walking his bike through the Las Olas crowd on Del Playa in the stained Carhartt, no costume — **he stops
dead under the Ferrante house's floodlights, looks at Theo** (tall, blond, a white dinner jacket, laughing on the porch) **and goes white**: the first
time he has seen the boy from the Point lot in the light. To Jack, passing: "Mr. Costigan." — not looking at him — "**Who is that?**" Jack's answer
(`e12_evan`: "My roommate. Theo. It's his party." / "Jay Gatsby. It's his party." / "Why?") is **the on-page route for E19's question** ("Your roommate
— was he at Preview Weekend last April?"); on `roommate` he has the name too. He rides off west toward Seacliff (🔒 never followed).

### 10.8 JACK'S FAMILY — the Costigans of Youngstown
Home: a narrow two-story frame house on **Glenwood Avenue near Brownlee Woods**, South Side, with a detached garage where
Frank keeps a space heater, a radio and a Stroh's fridge. Parish: **St. Brigid of Kildare** (fictional), South Side. Pastor:
**Fr. Dom Bruno** (named E06 plan; sixties, skipped the homily on Ephesians 5 the Sunday before Jack left — Aug 23, 2009).

- **Francis "Frank" Costigan, 51.** GM Lordstown assembly, 26 years, UAW Local 1112. Laid off since the GM bankruptcy
  (June 1, 2009); unemployment and the last of the SUB pay are running out by October. Drives a **1999 Chevy Silverado**
  (forest green, rust over the rear wheel wells, 212,000 miles). Sings Sinatra in the garage. Kennedy Democrat, pro-life,
  union to the marrow. Funny in a gruff, minimal way. Loves his kids ferociously and can't say it. 🔒 **Frank knows Jack
  threw the punch.** He brokered the plea: he went to Danny in the county jail and Danny said "Jackie's got the
  scholarship, Dad," and Frank let him. He has chosen one son's future over the other son's freedom, and he has to look at
  both of them. He drove Jack 2,400 miles because he couldn't afford two plane tickets and because he wanted to see the
  Pacific once before he died. He sleeps in the truck on the way home to save the motel money.
  - *"Close your mouth. You'll catch flies."* · *"Don't waste it."* · *"We don't. Not here. That's done."*
- **Maria Teresa (Rinaldi) Costigan, 49.** Secretary at St. Brigid's school. Devout, fierce, funny, tired. Visits Danny at
  Trumbull Correctional every other Sunday after the 8 a.m. Mass. 🔒 **Does not know** the truth. Believes Danny's version
  (a fight that went wrong). Her grief over Danny is the family's weather.
  - *"Did you eat? Don't lie to me, I can hear it in your voice when you haven't eaten."*
- **Daniel Francis "Danny" Costigan, 22** (b. Feb 2, 1987). 🔒 Serving five years at **Trumbull Correctional Institution**
  (Leavittsburg, Ohio) for felonious assault, pleaded January 2008. Eligible for judicial release consideration in 2011;
  release date 2012. He took the fall for Jack. He is funny, profane, gentle, does push-ups and reads every book Jack sends
  him (Jack sends one a month, used, with his own margin notes erased because the prison won't allow writing — Jack writes
  new notes on a separate sheet). In his letters he calls Jack "Professor." He does not resent Jack. That's the worst
  part for Jack.
  **(E06 plan:) Danny's first letter on the page** (lined paper, block capitals for the address, TRUMBULL CORRECTIONAL
  INSTITUTION and his inmate number on the envelope — Researcher to confirm the return-address format), written **Wed Sep
  16 night after he called the house and Gina said "fine,"** mailed Thursday, in Jack's Delgado box **Saturday**. It crosses
  the Thursday call: *"Tell Gina it ain't hers to carry. I got it."* (🔒 he is carrying Jack's; dramatic irony, never
  glossed) — which is what the system cut off ("tell Gina — "). Also: *"Tell the veil girl thanks for asking my name.
  Nobody out there asks it anymore. They ask Ma how I'm doing like I'm the weather."* Reggie from Portsmouth; the Hopkins
  ("Ma says it's poems. Send it anyway"). Signed **—D.** Danny's written voice: run-on, profane in a clean way, funny,
  generous; no self-pity; one sentence that stops Jack cold per letter.
  **(E08 plan:) Danny's second letter** — his answer to Jack's Sept 21 letter (which gave him Lena's name), in the Delgado box
  **Tue Sep 29, Michaelmas**: opens "Happy St. Mike's, Professor" (🔒 Danny was his confirmation sponsor; never stated); Reggie
  has decided the Pacific smells like "money and fish, which is California"; *Brideshead* is still with the C.O. ("he says it's
  about a house"); Gina told him on the Wednesday call that "the veil girl" has a boyfriend — "Gina says a lot of things. Tell
  Lena hey anyway. Lena. I like knowing it." The sentence that stops Jack cold: **"You sound happy out there. Don't feel bad about
  it. Somebody in this family ought to be. That's an order."** (🔒 the man carrying Jack's cross absolving him in advance, in the
  week Jack is guiltiest about something else; never glossed.) The Hopkins (margins erased — *Show Lena* erased by Lena herself,
  in the Delgado laundry room, Wed night) and Hale's Housman ship to Trumbull through Driftwood Thu Oct 1; Jack's reply says the
  Housman is "from a man who doesn't sleep" (`e7_housman=="sleep"`).
  **(E08:) Frank** — the Tuesday union-hall meeting (Local 1112): a recall list and talk of the small car (the Cruze) "next year,
  maybe"; Frank on the extension Saturday: "We'll see." (Researcher: keep to the public 2009 record — the Cruze was slated for
  Lordstown; no dates promised on the page.) **Rosie turns eight Wed Oct 7**; she wants "a crab" (her E06 drawing); Jack mails
  her a card Thursday.
- **Regina "Gina" Costigan, 15** (b. Apr 9, 1994). Sophomore at Ursuline High School. **(E05 canon:)** in sophomore
  religion, during the unit on the corporal works of mercy ("visit the imprisoned"), **Ashley Vrabel** says Gina's the class
  expert on the imprisoned; the room laughs; the teacher says "Ladies" and moves on. Ashley's father got a DUI last
  Christmas (South Side gossip; nobody was hurt — 🔒 the lucky driver). Smart-mouthed, Facebook-native,
  texts Jack constantly in lowercase. Bullied at school as "the convict's sister" (S1 subplot). Jack's favorite person to
  argue with. **(E07 plan:)** knowing the nun's name since E06, she friend-requests "Magdalena Broussard" on Facebook; Lena
  accepts and writes on Gina's wall (signed —L.); Gina finds Lena's "In a Relationship with Wesley Arceneaux" and Becca's
  two-exclamation-point post on Jack's wall, and says the obvious in lowercase. She does not tell Nonna. **Maria (E07, the
  Saturday call):** learns "Idaho" from Gina (Gina showed her Becca's profile picture) and is delighted ("Seven!"); then asks
  after the veil girl.
- **Rosaria "Rosie" Costigan, 7** (b. Oct 7, 2001 — Our Lady of the Rosary). Makes Jack drawings and mails them. Thinks
  California is made of beaches and Hannah Montana.
- **Nonna — Carmela (Ferraro) Rinaldi, 84.** Born in San Giovanni in Fiore, Calabria; came over 1951. Widow of Nonno
  Antonio Rinaldi, a Youngstown Sheet & Tube man who lost his job on **Black Monday, September 19, 1977**, and never
  recovered. Lives with the Costigans. Prays the rosary for Jack by name. Slips him twenties. Calls him "Giacomino."
  Early dementia beginning in S1 — **first on the page in E06** (Sat Sep 19, the phone): she asks *"E la ragazza?"* about
  the girl, gets her answer, and five minutes later asks it again in the same words, as new; Maria takes the phone back:
  "She's tired, honey." No gloss; Jack notices and tells only Lena (two-hander). 🔒 **Nonna knows.** She saw Jack's hand the morning after — the split knuckles, the
  stitched eyebrow, the blood under his nails — and said nothing. In S1E18 (Thanksgiving, St. Elizabeth's hospital) she says to him, in Italian, *"Tuo fratello
  porta la tua croce"* — your brother is carrying your cross. 🔒 Nonna dies in S3 (Lent 2011? or Advent 2010 — decide by
  path); Lena comes to the funeral in Youngstown.
  **(E08 plan:)** Maria told her the veil girl has a boyfriend (`e7_veil=="boyfriend"`): "Peccato." Then, sharp as a tack about
  the one thing: **"E l'altra?"** — and the other one? (Idaho.) Her memory for the Church calendar is perfect while the rest
  slips: on the Friday-sauce path she gives Jack the meatless *sugo* for a Friday ("Venerdì! Niente carne!") step by step, then
  asks who it's for, twice.
  **(E10 plan:)** **Danny's third letter** (in the box Tue Oct 13; no mail Columbus Day): the Hopkins came — "Felix Randal," Ma cried when he
  read it to her at the visit, "I'm telling you so you know I read it. Not so you feel bad"; the Housman — "tell your man who don't sleep that
  nobody in here does either. LIV made Reggie cry and Reggie is from Portsmouth"; "Ma says Idaho sent Rosie a crab. Ma says she's a nice girl.
  Ma says it how she says the Browns got a nice stadium."; "Did you tell Lena hey or do I got to come out there." (written before the curb); the
  sentence that stops Jack cold: **"Reggie asked me do I ever wish I did it different. I told him I don't wish. I decided. It's different."**
  (🔒 Danny chose to take it. Never glossed; Jack writes his paper's first sentence two hours later — "A man knows his own heart by what he says
  yes to" — and the page never connects them.) The **second money order** to D. Kostelnik ($20, the Mission Street post office, in the rain).
  **Maria** writes Becca a thank-you note for the crab (St. Brigid's stationery). Sat Oct 17: **Purdue 26, Ohio State 18** — Frank: "Purdue."
  "Thirteen-point favorite." That's the call. **Nonna on the extension: "Danny?" — "No, Nonna. È Giacomino." — "Giacomino. Sì. Lo so."** (🔒 *I
  know*; the step after E06/E08's repeats; never glossed) — then, sharp: "E la ragazza del granchio?"
  **(E11 plan:)** No Danny letter this week (his next answers Jack's "I told her hey" — E13–E14). Jack quotes him on the Big Sur porch: "My brother says he
  doesn't wish. He decides." (🔒 the meaning stays Danny's.) **Gina's text, read Sun Oct 25 on the Hermitage hill: *ashley vrabel says shes going to
  halloween as a prisoner. orange jumpsuit. she said its a TRIBUTE*** — the bully arc escalates toward E15 (`e5_gina_dui` waits). Jack may show Lena (she
  gives him a new line for Gina) or send Gina Lena's old advice (say his name; look till she looks away). Browns 3, Packers 31 (Oct 25; Researcher) —
  Frank, if on page: "Three." That's the call.
  **(E12 plan:)** Gina's Halloween (texts sent Sat night Eastern, read Sun 7:10 a.m. PT): she went **as a nun, "obviously"** (🔒 Nonna's word for
  Lena); **Ashley Vrabel wore the orange jumpsuit; Gina said Lena's line — VISIT, not dress up as — and Kelsey laughed at *Ashley*, "first time
  ever"**; then "ashley says im dead monday lol" (→ **E15 peak**, #19); "osu 45 nmsu 0. dad said who scheduled that" (Researcher). The same night, at
  the party, a Sigma Rho won Best Sinner as Madoff in an orange jumpsuit and Jack looked at the ice devil instead (🔒 Danny; the two jumpsuits never
  joined on the page).

### 10.9 LENA'S FAMILY — the Broussards of New Orleans
Home: a raised double shotgun converted to a single on **N. Dupre Street, Mid-City**, near Bayou St. John. Took 4½ feet
of water in 2005; rebuilt by Tony and his brothers by hand over two years; the waterline is still visible on the shed.
Parish: **Our Lady of the Holy Rosary** (Esplanade Ave.) — the family pews; Lena's First Communion; the Christmas Eve
crawl. Family business: **Broussard's Seafood**, a market on Banks Street.

- **Antoine "Tony" Broussard, 53.** Owns Broussard's Seafood. Loud, sentimental, a world-class griller and grudge-holder,
  Saints season-ticket holder since 1981 (through the Aints years, with a bag on his head in 1980 — family lore). Cajun
  roots in Breaux Bridge; talks to God like a business partner who owes him money.
  - *"Cher, you call your mama or I'm flying out there and I'm bringing the boudin."*
- **Celeste (Guidry) Broussard, 50.** Registered nurse; in 2005 an ICU nurse at **Memorial Medical Center** (Uptown);
  now at Touro Infirmary. Brisk, funny, ferociously competent, gives orders in a whisper. 🔒 **Her mother, Lucille Guidry
  (Lena's grandmother, "Grandmère"), was a long-term acute care patient on the hospital's seventh floor during Katrina, and
  was among those who died there before evacuation.** Celeste was working in the ICU downstairs. On the fourth day she was
  ordered onto a boat with other staff, and went, believing her mother would be evacuated within hours. She was not.
  Celeste has never told her family what she knew or didn't, what she asked or didn't ask, what she was told. The
  coroner returned Lucille's Miraculous Medal in a plastic bag in February 2006; Celeste gave it to Lena at fifteen
  without a word. Lena has never asked. Sheri Fink's long investigation of the Memorial deaths was published in *The New
  York Times Magazine* in late August 2009 — **the week of the pilot.** Celeste texts Lena: *"Don't read the Times this
  week, cher."* 🔒 *Writers' rule:* the show never asserts what any real person at Memorial did or did not do. The
  Broussards' grief is about **uncertainty, absence and a daughter's choice**. Celeste's own culpability, if any, is the
  choice to leave on the boat. The truth Celeste finally tells (adult seasons): she went back up the stairs before she
  left, and her mother was asleep, and she kissed her and left her, and she has wondered every day since if her mother
  ever woke.
- **Luc Broussard, 24.** Eldest; NOPD officer (joined 2006). Wes's best friend. Quiet, good, under pressure: in 2010 the
  federal Danziger Bridge indictments and other post-Katrina NOPD scandals tear at his department. 🔒 He knew things
  about his precinct's conduct in the storm's aftermath that he never reported — a mirror for Lena (S2–S3).
- **Thérèse "Tess" (Broussard) Fontenot, 22.** Married at 21 to Beau Fontenot, a lineman for Entergy; first baby due
  December 2009 (a girl, **Lucille**, born Dec 19, 2009 — Lena becomes godmother over Christmas break).
- **Remy Broussard, 20.** At LSU; party boy; Tiger Stadium; adores Lena; the family's comic disaster.
- **Jude and Josie Broussard, 13.** Twins. Wes carried them out of a neighbor's second floor in 2005. Jude is quiet; Josie
  is Lena in miniature.
- **Mémère — Odile Broussard, 88.** Tony's mother, Breaux Bridge, speaks Cajun French first, makes the best gumbo in
  Louisiana, thinks Jack is "un bon garçon mais trop maigre."
- **(E11 plan — Tony on the page, by phone, for the first time):** Sun Oct 25, Saints at Miami: Lena calls Dupre Street from the Hermitage hill and **Tony
  puts the phone on speaker beside the kitchen radio (WWL)** for three hours while the call drops at every curve of Highway 1 and Kevin pulls into every
  turnout with a bar. Loud, sentimental, a grudge-holder: "Who's driving? … Is he careful? … Good." **"Is that the Ohio boy? Wes says y'all got a Browns
  fan out there."** To Jack: "Browns? Lord, son. You need us more than we need you." Laney takes the phone ("Put the redhead on!"); Tony adores her on
  contact (S2's Mardi Gras makes it official). Lena buys **Mémère** a fruitcake from the monks. Luc, Remy, Tess and Josie flood her phone about the game.

### 10.10 CALEB HARGROVE — Hale's acolyte (added after S1E01)
Born **March 3, 1991** — 18. Visalia (Tulare County). Homeschooled until tenth grade, then Redwood High '09. Philosophy.
PHIL 9H. Portolá Hall.
**Who he is.** A Pentecostal (Assemblies of God) pastor's son, raised on tongues and the King James ("Selah," "verily," by
reflex). Deconverted at sixteen reading *The Consolations of Disbelief*; now Hale's most devoted reader — forty Post-its, a
signed copy, a Darwin fish on his laptop. Fast, funny, a little desperate, generous like a puppy; does tongues at parties as
a bit and looks sick afterward. 🔒 **Why he left:** at fifteen his father's church collapsed when the treasurer emptied the
building fund; the congregation scattered; his father's faith stayed untouched and serene, and Caleb could not forgive the
serenity (Hale's own wound a generation down; Hale will recognize it and use it). 🔒 He wrote Hale **four letters of
devotion; Hale never answered** — Hale writes only to believers who fight him. Caleb learns in S1E02 that Hale answered
Jack, and in S1E14 that he answered Lena. **Arc (MS):** S1 the boy Hale's book worked on, Jack's comic shadow; S2 Lena beats
Hale at the Forum and something in Caleb cracks; S3 he becomes Laney's study partner for the first actuarial exam (a deck of cards for the breaks; neither tells anyone); S8 (open) the
Easter Vigil — a back pew, gone before the Gloria. Never a strawman: his unbelief is honest grief, and he's right that his
father's church was a con for some of the people in it.
**Voice.** Central Valley twang, rapid, King James cadence used ironically; self-deprecating; "dude" in moments of terror.
- *"He wrote you back? I wrote him four letters. Four. I used a thesaurus on the third one."*

### 10.11 YUSUF NOORZAI — the third believer at the table (added after S1E01)
Born **November 12, 1990** — 18. Fremont, California (Little Kabul). Mission San Jose High '09. Aerospace engineering; PHIL
9H as his honors GE. Cabrillo Hall. Afghan-American, Sunni, devout and unshowy: finds a quiet stairwell for the prayers,
fasts Ramadan (Aug 22 – Sept 19, 2009 — he's fasting through Hale's first seminar), doesn't drink, plays cricket on the
Commons with grad students on Sundays. Dry, kind, precise; calls Jack and Lena "the Catholics"; Margot calls the three of
them "the Abrahamic bloc." Father **Ahmad**, a 1985 refugee by way of Peshawar, works the line at **NUMMI**, the GM–Toyota
plant in Fremont: GM pulled out in June 2009 and Toyota announced in late August it would close the plant in spring 2010
(Researcher to verify). Two sons of auto workers at Hale's table. Mother **Farida** runs a home daycare. Older brother
**Tariq**, 24, is a contract interpreter with the US Army in Helmand. 🔒 Tariq is wounded by an IED in late November 2009;
Yusuf gets the call the week of the West Point speech (S1E19). **Arc (SEA→MS):** S1 a friend at the table; E04 the
health-care night; E19 Tariq; S2 NUMMI closes (April 2010). The only arguments in the show where three people agree on
the first premise. Never a token: his own jokes, his own doubts (angrier at the Taliban than at God, and at America for
leaving the first time).
**Voice.** Bay Area understatement; deadpan; "inshallah" only when he means it; engineering metaphors ("load-bearing,"
"factor of safety"); a running bit about Catholic potlucks.
- *"I'm fasting, Professor. My soul is the only part of me that isn't hungry."*

### 10.12 RUDY SALCEDO — the Anchor's line cook (added after S1E01)
Born **March 30, 1966** — 43. Oxnard, then Marisol's Westside. Six years at Soledad (Correctional Training Facility) for
the armed robbery of a liquor store at nineteen (1985–1991; a starter pistol, a pint of Bacardi in him, nobody hurt).
Sober since 1994 (AA, Tuesday nights in St. Brendan's parish hall). Gloria hired him in 1995 when nobody would. Nights on
the line. A rosary tattooed around his left wrist (done inside); Vin Scully on a transistor; Louis L'Amour paperbacks in
his apron; the 9:30 at St. Brendan's, back row (he nods to Ray Ochoa; they don't talk). Gentle, slow, deadpan about the
dish machine ("Hobart. Older than you, meaner than Gloria"). Never volunteers prison, never hides it. His mother rode three
buses from Oxnard every other Sunday for six years. 🔒 **Role:** Jack's mirror for Danny; after E03 Jack can't look at
him for a while. E15: "I know what an errand is, mijo" (Rafi's offer). S8: Danny visits for graduation and he and Rudy talk
at the counter for an hour about nothing. Not a saint: a temper he manages like a stove.
**Voice.** Soft, unhurried, Westside Chicano; "mijo"; never raises it.
- *"First time I washed dishes was at Soledad. Six years. Best job in the building — it's warm, and nobody bothers the dish man."*

### 10.13 GIDEON STRAND — RETIRED (player's MAJOR RE-PLAN, after S1E11)
*(The organizer is dropped: **Lena has no magnetic guy.** He never reached the page; do not use the name Gideon Strand for
anyone. What survives of his S1 seed is Lena's own practice — the **Sunday soup for the Harbor Inn housekeepers** (Dulce and
Rosario; E10–E11, on the page; "Salt.") — which stays hers: Catholic Worker in deed, no organizer required. The proxy fights now
live in Jack's Sol period (§24.6); his near-miss, his "I can wait, Counselor," his 2012 masthead and Jack's "quiet thanks" to Theo
are retired. Jack's fear-as-POV about other men now attaches only to Julian, and is answered in S2E19. Brooks Callahan is not
restored as a rebound.)*
